{"id": "a56e39975ec58533", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T14:53:05.215642+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "example", "claim": "Radial crowding is NOT an obstruction to full complex-prefix legality: for n unit steps, z_n=1/(2n)+i/(10n) puts all n+1 prefix points in(1/2,1] in radius, yet one explicit 2D strongly convex interpolant and orthogonal terminal vector give error>1/(325n) for EVERY final step. The raw radial-density criterion fails here for every positive proposed strong-convexity parameter.", "evidence": "# Dense radial prefixes can still have an explicit strongly convex interpolant\n\nStatus: complete candidate argument, unverified; no new verifier allocation.\nThis is a diagnostic counterexample to treating radial crowding as a genuine\nlegality obstruction, and a new explicit interpolation tool. It does not\nsolve the unrestricted exact-profile comparison. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290\nis used only for maximal completion and its anchor representation.\n\n## 1. Direct interpolation on a Lipschitz graph\n\nLet finitely many planar points (s_i,t_i), including (0,0), lie on the graph\nt=phi(s) of a globally piecewise-linear continuous function phi with\nphi(0)=0 and Lipschitz constant ell>=0. Let z=alpha+i beta, with beta real,\nand suppose\n\n    mu=alpha-|beta|(2ell+1)>0,\n    L_g=alpha+|beta|(2ell+1)<=1.                               (1)\n\nDefine on the entire real plane\n\n    f(s,t)=alpha(s^2+t^2)/2 + beta s t\n                 -2beta integral_0^s phi(v) dv.                (2)\n\nThen f is mu-strongly convex, has L_g-Lipschitz gradient, is minimized at\nzero with value zero, and has\n\n    grad f(s_i,t_i)\n       =(alpha s_i-beta t_i, beta s_i+alpha t_i)\n       =z(s_i+i t_i).                                         (3)\n\nIndeed the gradient everywhere is\n\n    (alpha s+beta t-2beta phi(s), alpha t+beta s),\n\nwhich is continuous. On every linear piece of phi its Hessian is\n\n    [[alpha-2beta phi'(s), beta], [beta,alpha]].\n\nFor an arbitrary vector (a,b), the discrepancy of this quadratic form from\nalpha(a^2+b^2) has absolute value at most\n\n    2|beta|ell a^2 +2|beta||ab|\n       <=|beta|(2ell+1)(a^2+b^2).\n\nThus each Hessian lies between mu times the identity and L_g times the\nidentity. Integrating along any line segment, divided at the finitely many\nvertical break lines of phi, gives global strong convexity and the gradient\nLipschitz bound. A segment lying in a break line has constant s and the same\nbound directly from the gradient formula. Its gradient at zero is zero,\nso strong convexity makes zero the global minimizer. Substitution of\nt_i=phi(s_i) proves (3). In particular ALL ordered interpolation conditions\nhold for the actual values f(s_i,t_i); no value is assigned independently.\n\nIf these points are x_i=P_i(z), P_i the ordered schedule-prefix polynomial,\nthen (3) gives the actual GD recursion. Their Gram matrix is the ordinary\nreal Gram matrix in the plane. Rotating all points first is also allowed,\nsince scalar complex multiplication commutes with a planar rotation.\n\n## 2. Independent every-cycle estimate for the same graph geometry\n\nFor any simple directed cycle through these points, put Area_C and Energy_C\nas the signed area sum and squared-edge sum. Then\n\n    |Area_C| <= (ell/2) Energy_C.                              (4)\n\nTo prove this, write consecutive vertices as (a,phi(a)) and (b,phi(b)).\nThe area sum equals\n\n    sum_(a->b) (a-b)(phi(a)+phi(b)),\n\nsince its additional diagonal terms telescope. Also the oriented integrals\nsum_(a->b) integral_a^b phi(v)dv vanish. Piecewise integration by parts gives\n\n    (b-a)(phi(a)+phi(b))-2integral_a^b phi(v)dv\n        =integral_a^b (2v-a-b)phi'(v)dv.\n\nIts absolute value is at most ell(b-a)^2/2, in either orientation. Summing\nover the cycle and using (b-a)^2<=the squared Euclidean edge length proves\n(4). It has no dependence on the number of points in a radial annulus.\n\n## 3. An explicit family with every prefix point in one annulus\n\nFor every integer n>=1, take the n unit steps h_i=1 and define\n\n    z_n=1/(2n)+i/(10n),\n    c_n=1-z_n,\n    x_i=c_n^i=s_i+i t_i,      i=0,...,n,\n    g_i=z_n x_i,\n    x_star=g_star=0.\n\nAll these n+1 indexed nonzero prefix points have radius in (1/2,1].\nTo see this, r=|c_n| satisfies\n\n    r^2=1-1/n+13/(50n^2)<1,\n    r^n>(1-1/(2n))^n>=1/2.\n\nThe last inequality is Bernoulli's inequality, or follows by induction\nfrom (1-t)^n>=1-nt for0<=t<=1. Strictness follows from the nonzero imaginary\npart of c_n. Thus their radial density count for this annulus is n+1.\n\nNonetheless these points and the origin lie on a graph of Lipschitz constant\nell=2/5, uniformly in n. Let theta=-arg(c_n)>0. Then\n\n    n theta <= n/(10n-5)<=1/5.\n\nTherefore s_i>0, t_i<=0, and\n\n    rho_i=-t_i/s_i<=tan(1/5)<=2/5.\n\nHere tan(t)<=2t on[0,pi/4], by the derivative bound sec^2(t)<=2. The real\nparts strictly decrease because\n\n    s_(i+1)-s_i=-s_i[1/(2n)+rho_i/(10n)]<0.\n\nThe slopes of successive prefix chords are\n\n    (t_(i+1)-t_i)/(s_(i+1)-s_i)\n       =[(1/10)-(1/2)rho_i]/[(1/2)+(1/10)rho_i],\n\nwhose absolute values are at most1/5 for0<=rho_i<=2/5. The segment from\nthe origin to x_n has slope -rho_n of absolute value at most2/5. Define phi_n\nby piecewise-linear interpolation through the points in increasing real\ncoordinate order\n\n    (0,0), x_n,x_(n-1),...,x_0=(1,0),\n\nand extend it by zero outside[0,1]. This is one explicit globally continuous\npiecewise-linear function with Lipschitz constant at most2/5 and phi_n(0)=0.\n\nUse (2) with alpha=1/(2n), beta=1/(10n), and phi=phi_n. Formula(1) yields\n\n    mu=8/(25n),  L_g=17/(25n)<=1.                             (5)\n\nWe therefore have an explicit fixed convex1-smooth strongly convex function\nf_n, on the real plane, with exactly the displayed unit-step orbit and with\nall the stated real data simultaneously legal. This is not a formal moment\nmatrix or pairwise-only interpolation argument.\n\n## 4. One orthogonal terminal vector and a free-terminal lower value\n\nLet F_n be the maximal interpolant of these actual prefix jets, as provided\nby accepted1252b8823bb5a290. For j in I={star,0,...,n}, set\n\n    v_j=f_n(x_j), y_j=x_j-g_j, delta_j=v_j-|g_j|^2/2,\n    H(v)=|v|^2/2+max_(j in I)(<y_j,v>-delta_j), F_n=H^*.\n\nPointwise maximality and (5) give\n\n    H(v)<=f_n^*(v)<=|v|^2/(2mu).\n\nDefine D=x_n-(<x_n,g_n>/|g_n|^2)g_n and w=mu D. This same vector satisfies\n<w,g_n>=0 and |D|^2=|x_n|^2/26. EVERY anchor obeys\n\n    2delta_j+2<x_n-y_j,w>-|w|^2\n         >=2<x_n,w>-|w|^2/mu=mu|D|^2.\n\nConsequently, for every real b,\n\n    2F_n(x_n-bg_n)>=mu|D|^2\n           = [8/(25n)] |c_n|^(2n)/26\n           >1/(325n).                                       (6)\n\nWe used |c_n|^(2n)>1/4. The same bound follows for f_n itself directly from\nstrong convexity and the minimum distance from the origin to the line\nx_n-b g_n. Thus one fixed function handles every b; the endpoint gradient\nis unconstrained.\n\n## 5. Precise diagnostic consequence\n\nFor this legal spectral point, the sufficient radial-density test\n\n    (alpha-mu_0)(1-alpha)-beta^2\n        >=6M|beta|(1-mu_0),                                  (7)\n\nwith M=n+1, fails for EVERY proposed0<mu_0<1. If mu_0>=alpha its left side\nis negative. If mu_0<alpha, the left side is at most alpha=1/(2n)<=1/2,\nwhile the right side is greater than or equal to\n\n    (3/5)((n+1)/n)(1-1/(2n))>=3/5.\n\nThus radial crowding cannot be used to infer illegality or weakness of the\nfull complex-prefix ansatz. The complete graph interpolant above remains\nlegal and has an order1/n free-terminal payoff despite density n+1.\n\nUnit-step schedules already have stronger standard scalar lower witnesses;\n(6) is a diagnostic calibration, not a claimed improvement for them. The\nnew structural input is the graph geometry and its explicit nonseparable\ninterpolant, which avoids a point-count loss. Extending it to recurrent\nprefixes with multiple overlapping graph branches, while preserving all\nold gradients and values, remains open.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/dense_graph_orbit_interpolation.md", "related_candidates": ["cedcf8ce28d0fe89", "bc047aa498ba614a"], "status": "Complete diagnostic construction, unverified; no new fact_submit", "scope": "Explicit replacement for the radial count estimate on graph-like dense prefixes; not a new benchmark improvement on unit schedules."}, "glossary": {"phi": "Globally continuous piecewise-linear graph interpolation through the specified planar prefix and origin.", "ell": "Lipschitz constant of phi.", "L_g": "Gradient Lipschitz bound alpha+|beta|(2ell+1) of the explicit graph interpolant.", "mu": "Strong-convexity bound alpha-|beta|(2ell+1).", "z_n": "Spectral parameter1/(2n)+i/(10n) for the unit-step diagnostic.", "c_n": "Unit-step multiplier1-z_n.", "rho_i": "Negative imaginary-to-real ratio of the ith prefix point.", "w": "Strong-convexity parameter times the orthogonal projection of the last point away from its gradient.", "M": "Raw number of prefix points in an annulus; equalsn+1 in the diagnostic."}}
{"id": "8273524595704c66", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:13:52.236894+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "example", "claim": "Joint complex-mode repair is real: an explicit3D normal-operator prefix with h0=1 mixes z=1/2+(2/5)i and lambda=1/2 at weights1/2,1/2. The complex mode alone has positive interpolation cycle781/10000 and is impossible for ANY values; the joint data have all SIX Q strictly negative with f0=5/16,f1=3/40. One fixed maximal interpolant and w=(0,-1/(16sqrt2),0) give normalized error>=59/2560 for EVERY final step. This is a concrete calibration outside the newly excluded individual-mode class, not an arbitrary-horizon result.", "evidence": "# Exact joint repair of an individually illegal complex mode\n\nStatus: complete candidate calibration, unverified; no new fact_submit.\nAccepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 for interpolation/maximal completion\nand dfde2fa326c908bf for the complex cycle calculation. This is a concrete\nthree-dimensional object opening the remaining mixed-mode branch; it is\nnot a quantitative arbitrary-horizon profile theorem.\n\n## 1. A specified normal operator and the complete real prefix\n\nOn R^3 define the fixed real normal operator\n\n    A=[[1/2,-2/5,0], [2/5,1/2,0], [0,0,1/2]].\n\nThus its first plane is complex multiplication by z=1/2+(2/5)i and its last\ncoordinate is multiplication by lambda=1/2. Use the single prefix step\nh_0=1 and the exact real vectors\n\n    x_0=(1,0,1)/sqrt(2),\n    g_0=(1/2,2/5,1/2)/sqrt(2)=A x_0,\n    x_1=(1/2,-2/5,1/2)/sqrt(2)=x_0-g_0,\n    g_1=(41/100,0,1/4)/sqrt(2)=A x_1.\n\nThe squared spectral weights are1/2 and1/2, |x_0|^2=1, and all inner\nproducts come from this one real Euclidean Gram matrix. Include the optimum\njet x_star=g_star=0, f_star=0. Prescribe\n\n    f_0=5/16,  f_1=3/40.\n\nHere are the complete quantities needed to check legality:\n\n    |g_0|^2=33/100,\n    |g_1|^2=|g_0-g_1|^2=1153/10000,\n    <x_0,g_0>=1/2,\n    <x_1,g_1>=<g_0,g_1>=33/200.\n\nFor the signed interpolation residual\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+|g_i-g_j|^2/2, all SIX ordered inequalities are\n\n    Q_(0,star)=-9/400,      Q_(star,0)=-59/400,\n    Q_(1,star)=-647/20000,  Q_(star,1)=-347/20000,\n    Q_(0,1)=-697/20000,     Q_(1,0)=-297/20000.                (1)\n\nEach is strictly negative. These numbers follow by substituting the\ndisplayed inner products, x_1-x_0=-g_0, and f_0-f_1=19/80. Thus every\nordered smooth-convex interpolation condition holds on the SAME data.\n\n## 2. One spectral component is nevertheless illegal\n\nThe unweighted complex component has points\n\n    X_0=1, X_1=1-z=1/2-(2/5)i, X_star=0,\n    G_j=z X_j.\n\nHere alpha=1/2, beta=2/5, sigma=|z|^2=41/100, and\nkappa=alpha-sigma=9/100>0. Consider the cycle X_0->0->X_1->X_0. Its\nsigned area sum and edge energy are\n\n    Area=2/5,  Energy=1+41/100+41/100=91/50.\n\nThe exact interpolation cycle weight from accepteddfde2fa326c908bf is\n\n    beta Area-kappa Energy/2\n      =4/25-(9/100)(91/50)/2\n      =781/10000>0.                                         (2)\n\nTherefore NO function values can make this individual complex prefix\nlegal. Its pairwise cocoercivity coefficient kappa is positive, so the\nfailure is a genuine multi-point cycle, not merely a negative real part or\na violation of the two-point optimum condition.\n\nThe scalar component lambda=1/2 is individually legal, but the actual joint\nvalues in(1) are not obtained by a separable interpolation of the two\ncomponents: the first such component does not exist. Joint legality in(1)\nis therefore strictly more permissive than legality of every mode.\n\n## 3. Fixed maximal interpolant and a uniform free-terminal witness\n\nFor j in I={star,0,1}, put y_j=x_j-g_j and delta_j=f_j-|g_j|^2/2. Then\n\n    y_0=x_1, y_1=x_1-g_1, y_star=0,\n    delta_0=59/400, delta_1=347/20000, delta_star=0.\n\nDefine, explicitly,\n\n    H(v)=|v|^2/2+max_(j in I)(<y_j,v>-delta_j),\n    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).\n\nBy(1) and accepted1252b8823bb5a290, F is globally convex differentiable\n1-smooth, interpolates every listed jet, has minimum zero at zero, and is\nthe pointwise maximal interpolant of this fixed prefix.\n\nChoose the one fixed real terminal vector\n\n    w=(0,-1/(16sqrt(2)),0).\n\nIt satisfies\n\n    <w,g_1>=0,\n    |w|^2=1/512,\n    <x_1,w>=<y_0,w>=<y_1,w>=1/80.\n\nFor EVERY prefix anchor the affine lower score is therefore\n\n    star:  2delta_star+2<x_1-y_star,w>-|w|^2\n              =1/40-1/512=59/2560;\n    j=0:   2delta_0+2<x_1-y_0,w>-|w|^2\n              =59/200-1/512;\n    j=1:   2delta_1+2<x_1-y_1,w>-|w|^2\n              =347/10000-1/512.\n\nThe first is the minimum, since1/40<347/10000<59/200. Evaluating the\nconjugate at this same w, for any real b, gives\n\n    2F(x_1-b g_1)\n      >=2<x_1-b g_1,w>-2H(w)\n      =min_(j in I)[2delta_j+2<x_1-y_j,w>-|w|^2]\n      =59/2560.                                             (3)\n\nThe function, weights, prefix values and w are fixed before b is selected.\nNo final gradient has been forced to equal A(x_1-b g_1).\n\n## 4. The exact joint-cycle interface for further work\n\nFor any finite list of complex modes z_l=alpha_l+i beta_l and fixed weights\nw_l>=0 summing to one, define real direct-sum points and gradients by\n\n    x_i=(sqrt(w_l)P_i(z_l))_l,\n    g_i=(sqrt(w_l)z_l P_i(z_l))_l,\n    x_star=g_star=0,\n    kappa_l=alpha_l-|z_l|^2.\n\nThe complete interpolation edge weight is the sum of the individual edge\nweights. Consequently its cycle weight is exactly\n\n    sum_l w_l[beta_l Area_C(z_l)-kappa_l Energy_C(z_l)/2].       (4)\n\nThe joint data admit values if and only if(4)<=0 for EVERY directed cycle.\nNecessity follows by summing interpolation inequalities. For sufficiency,\nremove nonpositive cycles from paths to the optimum, let ell_i be the\nmaximum such path weight, set f_i=-ell_i, and apply the finite interpolation\nconstruction of accepted1252b8823bb5a290. This argument checks the joint\nvalues; it does not posit independent functions for each mode.\n\nEquation(4) is recorded as an interface, not as the missing quantitative\nanswer. The concrete data in Sections1--3 demonstrate that its additional\nfreedom is real. To repair the recursive-family obstruction, a chosen\nfinite family of modes and weights must control all cycles over the whole\nhorizon and retain a benchmark-scale free-terminal payoff. No such\narbitrary-horizon quantitative construction is established here.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf"], "artifact": "workers/max2/research/joint_illegal_mode_repair.md", "related_obstruction": "6e098f91c4a13444", "failure_synthesis": "687befe50820df21", "status": "Complete candidate calibration; unverified; no new fact_submit", "next_interface": "Control the complete integrated cycle weights and terminal anchor values over a growing horizon using a specified finite mode family."}, "glossary": {"A": "Explicit real normal3x3 operator with complex block1/2+(2/5)i and real block1/2.", "z": "Individually illegal complex mode1/2+(2/5)i.", "lambda": "Individually legal real mode1/2.", "w": "Fixed real terminal vector(0,-1/(16sqrt2),0), orthogonal to the last queried gradient.", "joint mode repair": "All interpolation inequalities hold for the summed real data although an individual spectral mode has a positive cycle.", "mode weights": "Fixed squared initial weights1/2 and1/2 in the example."}}
{"id": "892ca165436ef59c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-26T15:55:23.023900+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "example", "claim": "A genuinely2D elliptic repair survives the normal-mode obstruction locally: z=1/2+(2/5)i, T=diag(1,1/2), h0=1 has all6 interpolation residuals strictly negative and one fixed maximal interpolant with all-final-step payoff>=27/1280. The circular orbit at the SAME eigenvalues has positive triangle781/10000 and is impossible. Full metric-dependent all-cycle conditions and an exact recursive phase-averaging identity are included.", "evidence": "# Elliptic complex prefixes: exact repair and the full metric interface\n\nStatus: unverified candidate. This is a finite calibration and exact legality\ninterface, not the requested unbounded-horizon conclusion. Accepted facts\n1252b8823bb5a290 and dfde2fa326c908bf supply interpolation conventions; all new\ncalculations and the finite path argument are written out below.\n\n## 1. A legal elliptic prefix with an illegal circular counterpart\n\nTake the fixed real operator, initial vector and one-step schedule\n\n    A=[[1/2,-4/5],[1/5,1/2]],     x0=(1,0),      h0=1.\n\nIts eigenvalues are z=1/2+(2/5)i and conjugate(z). In fact A=T B T^(-1),\nwhere T=diag(1,1/2) and B=[[1/2,-2/5],[2/5,1/2]] is multiplication by z.\nThe full queried data are\n\n    g0=(1/2,1/5),        f0=17/50,\n    x1=(1/2,-1/5),      g1=(41/100,0),       f1=1/10,\n    x_star=g_star=0,    f_star=0.\n\nThe initial norm is one and x1=x0-g0, g_i=A x_i. Use\nQ_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2. Direct calculation gives\n\n    Q_(0,star)=-3/200,           Q_(star,0)=-39/200,\n    Q_(1,star)=-419/20000,       Q_(star,1)=-319/20000,\n    Q_(0,1)=-519/20000,          Q_(1,0)=-219/20000.\n\nFor example ||g0||^2=29/100, ||g1||^2=1681/10000, and\n||g0-g1||^2=481/10000. All six inequalities are strictly negative.\nAccepted1252b8823bb5a290 therefore constructs one fixed legal maximal\ninterpolant F of all three jets.\n\nSet w=(0,-1/16). It is orthogonal to g1. For every query or optimum anchor\nwrite y_j=x_j-g_j and delta_j=f_j-||g_j||^2/2. Here\n\n    y0=x1,          delta0=39/200,\n    y1=(9/100,-1/5),delta1=319/20000,\n    y_star=0,       delta_star=0.\n\nThe three terminal anchor expressions\n2delta_j+2<x1-y_j,w>-||w||^2 are respectively\n\n    39/100-1/256,\n    319/10000-1/256,\n    1/40-1/256=27/1280.\n\nThe last is the minimum, since319/10000>1/40. Thus for every final b>=0,\nthe SAME maximal interpolant and orthogonal vector give\n\n    2F(x1-b g1)>=27/1280.                                (1)\n\nNow use the CIRCULAR orbit with the same z and h0=1: initial point1,\ninitial gradient z, next point1-z, and next gradient z(1-z)=41/100.\nFor any putative values v0,v1, the optimum inequalities imply\n\n    v0<=59/200,          v1>=1681/20000,\n\nwhile the reverse adjacent inequality requires\n\n    v0-v1>=5781/20000.\n\nBut the first two permit at most4219/20000. The difference is\n1562/20000=781/10000>0. This is a positive triangle cycle, so no values\ncan realize the circular prefix. The elliptic repair uses no added mode or\ndimension; a different common real metric alone changes legality.\n\n## 2. All cycles for one fixed metric\n\nFor any nonnegative schedule h and complex z=alpha+i beta with beta!=0,\ndefine real coordinate vectors\n\n    r_i=(Re P_i(z),Im P_i(z)),\n    v_i=B r_i,         B=[[alpha,-beta],[beta,alpha]],\n    P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z),\n    r_star=v_star=0.\n\nChoose one real symmetric positive definite matrix M with M_11=1. A real\ninvertible T with T^T T=M exists by diagonalizing M and taking square roots\nof its positive eigenvalues. Set x_i=T r_i and g_i=T v_i. These are actual\nreal vectors, ||x0||=1, and the GD recursion holds for the supplied schedule.\n\nFor an ordered edge j->i define\n\n    q_ij=v_j^T M(r_i-r_j)+(v_i-v_j)^T M(v_i-v_j)/2.\n\nLet sym(X)=(X+X^T)/2. Then q_ij=tr(M C_ij), where\n\n    C_ij=sym((r_i-r_j)v_j^T)+(v_i-v_j)(v_i-v_j)^T/2.       (2)\n\nThus existence of legal values is equivalent to the simultaneous conditions\n\n    tr(M sum_(j->i in C) C_ij)<=0                         (3)\n\nfor EVERY directed simple cycle C on {star,0,...,n}. The same M is used\nthroughout; its three entries cannot vary with the cycle or the time index.\n\nHere is the complete sufficiency argument. If(3) holds, every closed walk\nhas nonpositive total edge weight by deleting successive simple cycles.\nFor each i let ell_i be the maximum q-weight of a directed path i->star.\nThe maximum exists among simple paths, because removing a nonpositive cycle\ndoes not lower a path weight. In particular ell_star=0. Put f_i=-ell_i.\nAppending an edge j->i to a maximizing path from i to star gives\nell_j>=q_ij+ell_i, hence f_i>=f_j+q_ij. These are exactly all the required\nordered smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. The accepted maximal\nconstruction produces a real fixed legal F. Conversely summing such value\ninequalities around any cycle proves(3).\n\n## 3. A geometric form that exposes the anisotropy\n\nDefine the real symmetric matrices\n\n    S_M=(B^T M+M B)/2,\n    K_M=S_M-B^T M B.\n\nFor a cycle C let Area_C=sum_(j->i in C) det(r_j,r_i). Expanding the edge\nand telescoping its symmetric potential gives the exact identity\n\n    sum_C q_ij\n       =[beta tr(M)/2] Area_C\n          -(1/2)sum_C(r_i-r_j)^T K_M(r_i-r_j).             (4)\n\nTo check the skew term, put J=[[0,-1],[1,0]]. Then\nB=alpha I+beta J, JM+MJ=tr(M)J, and\n(B^T M-MB)/2=-beta tr(M)J/2. Since r_j^T J r_i=-det(r_j,r_i), its\ncontribution is exactly the first term of(4). The symmetric part of each\nedge is half the difference of r_i^T S_M r_i and r_j^T S_M r_j, minus\nhalf the quadratic increment; the potential differences cancel on a cycle.\n\nThe reversed cycle has the same quadratic increments and the opposite\narea. Thus the full condition is equivalently\n\n    sum_C(r_i-r_j)^T K_M(r_i-r_j)\n                  >= |beta| tr(M) |Area_C|               (5)\n\nfor every unoriented simple cycle. In particular every two-point cycle\nrequires (r_i-r_j)^T K_M(r_i-r_j)>=0. This is only positivity on actual\nchord directions; it does not imply K_M is positive semidefinite.\n\nFor explicit parameter calculations,\n\n    K_M=(alpha-alpha^2)M\n        +beta(1/2-alpha)(M J-J M)-beta^2 adj(M),\n    tr(K_M)=(alpha-|z|^2)tr(M),                           (6)\n\nwhere adj(M) is the2x2 adjugate. The identity J M J=-adj(M) verifies(6).\nWhen M=I, (5) reduces exactly to the circular area/energy condition in\naccepteddfde2fa326c908bf.\n\nFor the explicit repair in Section1, M=diag(1,1/4), alpha=1/2, beta=2/5,\nand\n\n    K_M=diag(21/100,-39/400).\n\nIt has a strictly negative eigenvalue, but its quadratic form is positive\non every actual nonzero chord: the horizontal chord gives21/100 and the\ntwo remaining chord directions(+/-1/2,2/5) give369/10000. The all-cycle\ntest is satisfied as certified by the six explicit value inequalities.\nReplacing actual chord positivity by global positive semidefiniteness of\nK_M would incorrectly discard this legal example.\n\n## 4. Recursive copies preserve one common metric\n\nFor H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), let p_j be its full polynomial and\nR_j=p_j(z)(1-b_j z). Induction on the actual ordered queries shows that\nthe multiset of positions of H_m is\n\n    { product_(j=0)^(m-1) R_j^(epsilon_j) : epsilon_j in{0,1} }.\n\nThe left copy has the old positions, while the right copy multiplies them\nall by R_(m-1). This identity retains multiplicities and query chronology\nwhen the binary choice is read from the recursive tree.\n\nFix a cycle inside H_r. Every copy of H_r in H_m is obtained by multiplying\nits coordinates by a product of R_j for j>=r. A nonreal z and real steps\nmake all these factors nonzero. If their product has argument theta and\nmodulus d, divide that copy's legal cycle inequality by d^2. It becomes\nthe SAME cycle inequality with metric R_theta^T M R_theta, where R_theta\nis planar rotation. Averaging over all2^(m-r) copies is therefore legitimate\nand retains a positive combination of actual full-prefix inequalities.\n\nThe isotropic part tr(M)I/2 is unchanged by this averaging. The two-dimensional\ntraceless part is rotated by twice each argument, and its norm is multiplied\nby\n\n    product_(j=r)^(m-1) |cos(arg R_j)|.                   (7)\n\nThis follows from averaging identity and rotation at each level: in a complex\ncoordinate for traceless symmetric matrices the multiplier is\n(1+exp(2i arg R_j))/2, whose modulus is |cos(arg R_j)|. Formula(7) is an\nexact possible bridge to a quantitative obstruction, not a claimed decay\nbound. Resonant phase choices must be analyzed rather than silently excluded.\n\n## 5. Retrieval and open quantitative step\n\nMatlas returned unrelated statements in arXiv:1310.0717,1810.05847 and\n1410.0486; none is used. No external theorem is imported. The fully legal\ncalibration is only one step; it does not establish a growing-horizon payoff.\nThe new task is to determine whether the metric repair persists quantitatively\non a specified recursive family. Formula(7) suggests testing phase averaging,\nincluding exact resonances, while(5) keeps ALL cycles active on one metric.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max2_elliptic_complex_metric", "artifact": "workers/max2/research/elliptic_complex_metric_calibration.md", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "dfde2fa326c908bf"], "status": "Complete finite calibration and structural interface, unverified; unbounded-horizon payoff not established", "scope": "One complex eigenpair and one fixed positive definite2x2 metric, normalized M_11=1; no arbitrary Gram kernel.", "new_interface": "Each recursive-copy average attenuates the traceless metric by product|cos(arg R_j)|. {"id": "db5c6ee920bcc660", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T09:35:01.650226+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "example", "claim": "C91 complete UNVERIFIED joint construction: on EVERY original H_m, m>=4, an explicit positive full-support D=2^(m+1) character law with original-index/sign labels has DENSE genuinely signed queried correlations and original-B payoff B_n V>1/150. No queried pair is orthogonal. This is a strictification/lift of the accepted scalar first-cut calibration, NOT an improved lower bound or arbitrary-h matching result.", "evidence": "# C91: a dense signed character law with a complete static realization\n\nStatus: **complete joint draft, UNVERIFIED**. This is the second newly\nauthorized analytical round, completed count23 after baseline21, reaching\nceiling23. The historical interruption is separate (attempted count24).\nNo computation, numerical evaluation, scan, replay, solver, installation,\nsubagent, or verifier call was used. The correctness predecessors are\naccepted `1252b8823bb5a290` and `b98bb807d514e80d`. No assertion in the\nunverified C90 manuscript is used as a proof premise.\n\nThis is a concrete new dense signed law/label geometry and a calibration\nof its actual payoff. It does NOT improve the already accepted scalar\nsingle-cut lower bound, prove an iterable improving child rule, or resolve\nthe original arbitrary-schedule exact-profile problem.\n\n## 1. Original word and quantitative claim\n\nFix m>=4 and retain, throughout the whole construction,\n\n    N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2), delta=1-1/m,\n    c_chr=sigma(1+1/m), a_chr=delta/c_chr,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_chr*c_chr^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nUse exactly h=H_m. Thus h_i=1+a_chr*c_chr^(nu_2(i+1)), 0<=i<n.\nEvery child has the same enclosing-m parameters. There is no reset,\npadding, schedule permutation, or replaced bridge. For the proof write\n\n    a=a_chr, ell=h_0=1+a, S=sum_(i<n)h_i,\n    T=S-ell, q=a/(2T+1),\n    mu=q/[64(1+S)^2].                                  (1)\n\nHere a is a local abbreviation for a_chr, not the earlier single-cut\nslope notation in the project glossary. The elementary bounds are\n\n    6/25<a<1/2, ell<3/2, T>=1, 0<q<=a/3<1/6,\n    0<mu<1/64, mu S<1.                                 (2)\n\nAll physical steps exceed one. The claim is an explicit convex\ndifferentiable 1-smooth F_dense, with unique minimizer0, whose actual GD\non this word has true initial radius R_dense and satisfies\n\n    V_dense=2F_dense(X_n)/R_dense^2\n          >=(3/4) a^2/(2T+1),\n    B_n V_dense>1/150.                                 (3)\n\nThe constant in (3) holds for EVERY m>=4 on the original words above.\nIt is a positive calibration, not the requested asymptotic factor one.\n\nIts queried gradients have a strictly positive finite character law and\ndense signed Gram matrix. In particular every later queried gradient has\nnegative correlation with g_0, and no two nonzero queried gradients are\northogonal. This removes both premises behind the C89 selection mechanism.\n\n## 2. Why simply adding independent coordinates fails\n\nThe scalar asymmetric Huber function\n\n    phi_q(x)=x^2/2                     if x>=-q,\n             -q x-q^2/2               if x<=-q          (4)\n\nhas continuous nondecreasing 1-Lipschitz derivative, unique minimizer0,\nand is an instance of the accepted single-cut construction. From x_0=1,\nthe original first step ell sends it to -a. Its remaining gradients are\nall -q and its final value has doubled gap a q=a^2/(2T+1).\n\nOn two different tail samples i<j, both ordered scalar interpolation\nresiduals are zero because that branch is affine. If one replaces a scalar\ngradient z_i by (sqrt(1-epsilon)z_i,sqrt(epsilon)|z_i|e_i), uses the actual\nsame-step positions, and simply scales all values by 1-epsilon, the reverse\nresidual on such a pair becomes epsilon q^2>0. Thus the naive full-support\nlift is NOT interpolable. This is an exact original-row obstruction to\nthat shortcut, for every epsilon>0.\n\nThe next sections supply and price the missing strict slack before adding\nthe independent coordinates.\n\n## 3. A strict scalar function on the actual schedule\n\nDefine one scalar function, fixed before the run,\n\n    f_mu(x)=(1-2mu)phi_q(x)+mu x^2/2.                  (5)\n\nIt is convex, differentiable, and has derivative Lipschitz constant\n1-mu<1. Its curvature where defined is either mu or 1-mu. Its unique\nminimizer is0. Write x_i for its actual GD trajectory from x_0=1 and\nz_i=f_mu'(x_i). The first actual block is the physical leaf ell:\n\n    z_0=1-mu,\n    x_1=1-ell(1-mu)=-a+mu ell.                        (6)\n\nFor comparison define the unmodified Huber tail locations\n\n    x_i^0=-a+q sum_(t=1)^(i-1)h_t, 1<=i<=n.\n\nThey lie in [-a,-(a+q)/2], so are at most -a/2. We now prove that all\nactual x_i, i>=1, remain on the NEGATIVE FLAT branch of phi_q. Although\nphi_q is flat in its derivative there, f_mu has the small positive\ncurvature mu, which is essential.\n\nPut D_mu=2mu(1+S)=q/[32(1+S)]<q/32. As long as x_i<=-q, the true recursion is\n\n    z_i=-(1-2mu)q+mu x_i,\n    x_(i+1)=(1-mu h_i)x_i+(1-2mu)q h_i.              (7)\n\nWriting d_i=x_i-x_i^0, it becomes\n\n    d_1=mu ell,\n    d_(i+1)=(1-mu h_i)d_i-mu h_i(2q+x_i^0).          (8)\n\nAll coefficients 1-mu h_i are nonnegative. Also\n|2q+x_i^0|<=a+2q<1. Induction in (8) therefore gives\n|d_i|<=mu(ell+sum_(1<=t<i)h_t)<=mu S<D_mu. Its own branch assumption is\npreserved, since\n\n    x_i<=-a/2+D_mu<-a/2+q/32\n                       <=-47a/96<-q.                (9)\n\nThis proves (7)-(9) for every original sample, including n; it is not a\ncalculation on a reset or compressed tail. The closed formula, if desired,\nis x_i=D+(x_1-D)product_(t=1)^(i-1)(1-mu h_t), where\nD=(1-2mu)q/mu. Equations (6)-(7) specify all data without an optimization.\n\nFor i>=1 the gradients z_i are negative. Their magnitudes A_i=|z_i|\nsatisfy q/2<=A_i<=2q<1, using (2), (9), and mu<q. Also A_0=1-mu<=1.\nEvery tail step increases x_i by at least q/2 because h_i>1.\nConsequently every pair of distinct sampled scalar positions, INCLUDING\nthe optimum0, is separated by at least q/2. For pairs involving the\noptimum use |x_i|>=47a/96>q for i>=1 and x_0=1; for a pair involving\nx_0 and a tail position use their opposite signs.\n\nThe actual doubled terminal value retains a quantitative fraction of the\nknown scalar payoff. Since both x_n and x_n^0 lie in the branch (4),\n\n    2f_mu(x_n)>=(1-2mu)[a q-2q D_mu]\n               >=(1-2mu)[a q-q^2/16]\n               >=(31/32)(47/48)a q>(3/4)a q.         (10)\n\nThis explicitly prices the regularization and the change in trajectory.\n\n## 4. Uniform strict slack for EVERY ordered scalar pair\n\nFor any scalar arguments x,y, put d=y-x and e=phi_q'(y)-phi_q'(x),\nand set alpha=1-2mu. The necessary inequality in accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290` applied to phi_q says\n\n    phi_q(y)-phi_q(x)-phi_q'(x)d>=e^2/2.\n\nFor f_mu, its gradient difference is alpha e+mu d. Expanding its signed\nordered residual gives\n\n    Q_mu(x,y)\n      =f_mu(x)-f_mu(y)+f_mu'(x)d\n                            +[f_mu'(x)-f_mu'(y)]^2/2\n      <=-alpha e^2/2-mu d^2/2+(alpha e+mu d)^2/2\n       =-alpha mu(e-d/2)^2-mu d^2/4\n      <=-mu d^2/4.                                    (11)\n\nThis holds in BOTH orientations, for every x,y, including zero.\nCombining the actual sample separation with (11), define\n\n    nu=mu q^2/16>0,\n    epsilon=nu/[4(1+nu)] in (0,1/4).                  (12)\n\nEvery original scalar residual between distinct samples in\n{star,0,...,n}, with star=(0,0,0), is at most -nu. Thus the smallest actual\nnonempty block (6), its optimum incidences, both directions of every later\npair, and every terminal pair have a common explicit negative margin.\n\nThe displacement in (11) is the actual scalar displacement, equivalently\nd=-sum h_t z_t between the corresponding chronological samples. The\nsigned contributions from the initial positive gradient and the later\nnegative gradients have not been changed or deleted.\n\n## 5. Dense vector lift with all original pairs\n\nWork initially in the Euclidean space R direct-sum R^N. Let e_i,\n0<=i<=n, be its standard orthonormal vectors in the second summand.\nPrescribe the full data by\n\n    G_i=(sqrt(1-epsilon) z_i, sqrt(epsilon) A_i e_i),\n    X_0=(sqrt(1-epsilon),0),\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t G_t,\n    f_i=(1-epsilon)f_mu(x_i),                         (13)\n\nand include the optimum jet (0,0,0). The first coordinate of X_i is\nsqrt(1-epsilon)x_i. Its second coordinate is\nsqrt(epsilon)Z_i, where Z_i=-sum_(t<i)h_t A_t e_t.\n\nThe original full residual splits EXACTLY, with the full signed scalar\ndisplacement intact, as\n\n    Q_ij=(1-epsilon)Q_mu(x_i,x_j)+epsilon R_ij.        (14)\n\nFor i<j finite, the added-coordinate residuals are\n\n    R_ij=(1/2-h_i)A_i^2+A_j^2/2,\n    R_ji=(A_i^2+A_j^2)/2.                            (15)\n\nFor an optimum pair, R_(i,star)=R_(star,i)=A_i^2/2,\nsince the i-th coordinate of Z_i is zero. As every A_i<=1 and h_i>=0,\nALL these residuals are at most one. Thus (11)-(12) prove the uniform\nstrict original-pair bound\n\n    Q_ij<=-(1-epsilon)nu+epsilon=-3nu/4<0             (16)\n\non every distinct ordered pair of {star,0,...,n}. In particular (15) at\nindices0,1 resolves the very first original physical block with its\ngenuinely negative correlation, before any appeal to a larger block.\n\nThis is a full pairwise calculation, not a covariance feasibility test.\nThe Gram and anchor products are\n\n    <G_i,G_j>=(1-epsilon)z_i z_j+epsilon A_i^2 1_(i=j),\n    <X_0,G_i>=(1-epsilon)z_i, ||X_0||^2=1-epsilon.    (17)\n\nFor all i>=1, <G_0,G_i><0. For every distinct pair, the product in (17)\nis nonzero. Accordingly there is no orthogonal queried pair and no\nnontrivial orthogonal component partition. The C89 history sign condition\nfails, and its large orthogonal selection is absent. The negative skipped\nterms are precisely (1-epsilon)h_t z_i z_t, which remain in (14).\n\n## 6. An explicit positive finite character law and chronological labels\n\nThe same geometry is also a literal finite character construction.\nUse d=m+1 bits and D_law=2^d=2N. Give physical sample i the label\n\n    lambda_0=(0,binary(0)),\n    lambda_i=(1,binary(i)), 1<=i<=n.                  (18)\n\nThe first bit records the sign, the other m bits record the original\nphysical index. The labels are distinct. They do not reorder or duplicate\nany sample or insert any step.\n\nLet s range over {0,1}^d, write e_sig for the vector with its first bit1\nand all other bits0, and define\n\n    chi_s(lambda)=(-1)^(s dot lambda),\n    w_s=(1-epsilon)1_(s=e_sig)+epsilon/D_law.           (19)\n\nEvery weight is strictly positive and their sum is one. Define\nu_i=(sqrt(w_s)chi_s(lambda_i))_s, and let eta_0=1, eta_i=-1 for i>=1.\nFinite character orthogonality gives, with no positivity inference from\nfinite-field algebra,\n\n    <u_i,u_j>=(1-epsilon)eta_i eta_j+epsilon 1_(i=j).   (20)\n\nTherefore the actual gradients g_i=A_i u_i have exactly (17). This law\nhas dense correlation support: for every nonzero binary label difference\nthe kernel is (1-epsilon)(-1)^(its first bit), never zero.\n\nFor an entirely explicit anchor, including its true norm, put\n\n    D_0=epsilon+(1-epsilon)N,\n    lambda_extra=(0,binary(1)),\n    v_s=chi_s(lambda_extra)/(D_law sqrt(w_s)),\n    v_unit=v/||v||,\n    Xhat_0=(1-epsilon)/D_0 sum_(i=0)^n eta_i u_i\n                 +sqrt(epsilon(1-epsilon)/D_0) v_unit. (21)\n\nThe label lambda_extra is not one of (18). Pairing a differing bit proves\n<v,u_i>=0 for every i. Also\nsum_i eta_i<u_i,u_j>=D_0 eta_j. Equations (20)-(21) give\n\n    <Xhat_0,g_i>=(1-epsilon)z_i,\n    ||Xhat_0||^2=(1-epsilon)^2 N/D_0\n                         +epsilon(1-epsilon)/D_0\n                   =1-epsilon.                      (22)\n\nThus the anchor and gradients in (21) have the identical FULL Gram data\nof (13), including the initial point. Set\nXhat_i=Xhat_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and retain the values f_i in (13).\nAll original residuals (14)-(16) hold verbatim. The term involving v_unit\nis an explicitly priced orthogonal component of the anchor, not an\nunrecorded radius change. The dimension increase changes no time index.\n\n## 7. ONE static function, exact values, unique minimizer, and true radius\n\nUse either the direct-sum realization (13) or the character realization\n(18)-(22); write X_i,g_i for the chosen positions and gradients. Define\n\n    y_i=X_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,\n    H_dense(v)=||v||^2/2\n                    +max{0,max_(0<=i<=n)(<y_i,v>-delta_i)},\n    F_dense(X)=sup_v(<X,v>-H_dense(v)).                (23)\n\nThe optimum row of (16) gives delta_i>=3nu/4>0. By accepted\n`1252b8823bb5a290`, the complete pair system (16) and formula (23) produce\nan everywhere finite convex differentiable 1-smooth function satisfying\nF_dense(X_i)=f_i and grad F_dense(X_i)=g_i at EVERY supplied sample.\nIts actual GD is therefore exactly the original physical trajectory.\nThis uses one fixed function for the whole horizon, not changing\nfunctions at a bridge.\n\nSince every delta_i is strictly positive, H_dense(v)=||v||^2/2 in some\nneighborhood of zero. It is everywhere at least ||v||^2/2, so\nF_dense(0)=0. For any X!=0 choose a sufficiently small 0<t<1; then\nH_dense(tX)=t^2||X||^2/2, implying\nF_dense(X)>=(t-t^2/2)||X||^2>0. Hence\n\n    argmin F_dense={0},\n    R_dense^2=||X_0||^2=1-epsilon,\n    V_dense=2(1-epsilon)f_mu(x_n)/(1-epsilon)\n                =2f_mu(x_n).                         (24)\n\nThus the dense character lift preserves the strict scalar instance's\nnormalized payoff EXACTLY. The only loss from the known Huber calibration\nis the already bounded scalar strictification (10). This proves the first\ninequality in (3), with actual values, minimizer and radius supplied.\n\nFor original smoothness L, replace F_dense by L F_dense and use physical\nGD steps h_i/L. Scaling X by R_dense yields initial distance one if desired\nand preserves all normalized claims.\n\n## 8. Comparison with the ORIGINAL exact benchmark\n\nHere B_n is precisely the accepted Zhang--Jiang denominator\n2S_bullet(n)+1, not 1+sum h or a newly defined profile. Recall from\naccepted `b98bb807d514e80d` the original mass recurrences\n\n    C_0=B_0=1,\n    C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),\n    Omega(u,v)=[u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv)]/2,\n    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2.                    (25)\n\nBoth joins are increasing. Also G(u,v)>=Omega(u,v): after cancelling\nu+v from twice both expressions, it suffices to square the nonnegative\nquantities 3u+sqrt(v^2+8uv) and sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2). The first square minus\nthe second is 8u^2+2uv+6u sqrt(v^2+8uv)>=0. Induction in (25) therefore\ngives B_n>=C_n. Choosing the balanced split repeatedly, and using\nOmega(u,u)=sigma u, gives\n\n    B_(2^m-1)>=C_(2^m-1)>=sigma^m.                    (26)\n\nThe sum of the ACTUAL word has the exact closed form\n\n    S=N-1+a_chr(c_chr^m-N)/(c_chr-2)\n     =N-1+r_sum(c_chr^m-N),\n    r_sum=delta/[c_chr(c_chr-2)].                      (27)\n\nIndeed there are 2^(m-k-1) occurrences of level k. Summing their finite\ngeometric series yields (27), without changing any enclosing parameter.\nSince c_chr>sigma, c_chr^2-2c_chr>1>delta, whence 0<r_sum<1 and\n\n    S<c_chr^m-1,\n    2T+1<2c_chr^m,\n    c_chr^m=sigma^m(1+1/m)^m<3sigma^m.                (28)\n\nFor the last inequality expand the binomial: each coefficient after\ndivision by m^k is at most 1/k!, and sum_(k>=0)1/k!<3 because\nk!>=2^(k-1) for k>=1, with strict inequality for some k.\n\nCombining (10), (24), (26)-(28), and a>6/25 gives\n\n    B_n V_dense>(3/4) a^2/6=a^2/8\n                  >9/1250>1/150.                    (29)\n\nFor a>6/25, use a=(sqrt(2)-1)(m-1)/(m+1),\n(m-1)/(m+1)>=3/5, and sqrt(2)>7/5, all valid for m>=4.\n\nThis proves a positive original-B payoff for the specified dense signed\nlaw family on every original enclosing horizon. It does not prove a\nmatching factor, and it says nothing comparable for arbitrary h solely\nfrom success on these particular words.\n\n## 9. Explicit costs and the remaining mathematical gap\n\nThe new construction uses d=m+1 binary label bits, D_law=2N character\ncoordinates, and the exact anchor (21). Its positive mixture mass is\n\n    nu=mu q^2/16=q^3/[1024(1+S)^2],\n    epsilon=nu/[4(1+nu)].                             (30)\n\nThe smallest probability atom is epsilon/D_law. It may be very small;\nno assertion that it stays uniformly positive is made. The original-pair\nslack is at least 3nu/4. Its actual function's minimizer is unique, its\ninitial squared radius is exactly 1-epsilon, and its payoff is exactly\nthe strict scalar payoff. These costs are explicit, not asymptotic\nnotation hiding a lost radius factor.\n\nThe full signed history is essential: z_0>0 while every z_i<0 for i>=1.\nIts queried graph is connected and has no orthogonal pair. Its physical\nsign labels (18) differ from standard binary chronological labels. Both\nthe law and the queried geometry therefore lie outside C89's completed\npositive-history family and outside a bounded-component obstruction.\n\nFor coordination with max's changed queried-child geometry, the complete\nboundary data here are (17), (22), all actual values (13), and strict\nresiduals (16). Every optimum-facing intrinsic height\nf_i-<g_i,X_i>+||g_i||^2/2 is strictly negative, rather than zero. This is\nuseful scope information; it is NOT a proved affine child-join operation\nor a capacity surplus for max's construction.\n\nTwo false extrapolations have concrete answers. The naive zero-slack\northogonal perturbation is refuted by Section2. Conversely, the claim\nthat every positive full-support signed character construction has\nvanishing original-B payoff is refuted, at joint-draft level, by (29).\nNeither claim is the original minimax conjecture.\n\nThe decisive remaining gap is improving the underlying actual scalar or\nmultidimensional trajectory enough to match the benchmark for arbitrary\nschedules. Strictification and the character lift PRESERVE a given\nscalar payoff; they do not create a better one. The accepted single-cut\nfunctional is already known to miss the benchmark on other schedules\n(see `b98bb807d514e80d`). Thus merely optimizing the perturbation epsilon\ncannot close the target.\n\nThe proof was developed by direct proving and the construct-counterexamples\ntest of the naive lift. Matlas was queried for the strictification identity;\nthe returned generic convexity statements were not used as premises. The\nexact Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur interpolation paper and proof had been\nread in this analytical session; the needed construction is already\naccepted1252b8823bb5a290. {"id": "f3d47cc96b3c6891", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:03:14.466730+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "example", "claim": "Actual uncovered signed freedom on the SAME original H_m: a single strictly curved quadratic has initial gradient signs +,-,+, so C92's one-initial-sign assumption is a real restriction. Full values, all Q pairs, unique minimizer and true radius are explicit. This is a scope test only, not an asymptotic lower-bound claim.", "evidence": "For every m>=4 keep exactly the original H_m and its parameters a=(sqrt2-1)(m-1)/(m+1), c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m). Put ell=h0=1+a, kappa=(1+1/ell)/2 and F(x)=kappa*x^2/2. Then0<kappa<1, argmin F={0}, and start x0=1 has true radius1. For every original sample i let x_i=product_(t<i)(1-kappa*h_t), g_i=kappa*x_i and f_i=kappa*x_i^2/2, including all physical steps and the free terminal. Direct expansion of the original residual gives Q_ij=-kappa*(1-kappa)*(x_i-x_j)^2/2<=0 for EVERY ordered pair, including the optimum sample0. Moreover kappa*h0=(ell+1)/2>1, while h1=1+(1-1/m)>ell, so kappa*h1>1. The unchanged complete physical hierarchy\n\nFix ANY integer m>=7 and set\n\n    u=1/(4m), lambda=1+sqrt2,\n    beta=1-u, c=lambda*(1+u), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a*c^l,H_l),   0<=l<m,\n    b_l=1+a*c^l, h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S_l=sum H_l.\n\nEvery child uses this SAME enclosing m and tau=1/4. There is no prefix\nreset, child restart, step permutation, omitted update, or padding.\nEvery original h_t>1 is retained. Its exact index is\n\n    h_t=b_(nu_2(t+1)),     0<=t<n,\n\nwhere nu_2(j) is the largest nonnegative integer exponent with2^nu_2(j)\ndividing the positive integer j. Induct on H_(l+1): the center has\nt+1=2^l, and right-child indices use\nnu_2(2^l+j)=nu_2(j) for0<j<2^l.\n\nDefine v_h=a/(c-2). The full physical mass is exactly\n\n    1+S_l=v_h*c^l+(1-v_h)*2^l.                         (1)\n\nThe induction is1+S_(l+1)=2(1+S_l)+a*c^l. Here beta>=27/28,\n2<c<3, a<1/2 and0<v_h<1: c-2>lambda-2=1/lambda>a.\nAlso a=beta/c>9/28, so v_h>a>9/28. All h_t>1 give S_m>n.\n\nFor the first law put\n\n    M=2+a, s=M-1=1+a, E=beta*c, Delta=E-M.\n\nThe cancellation is exact:\n\n    Delta=2u/[lambda*(1+u)]-lambda*u^2,\n    u/2<Delta<2u<=1/14.                              (2)\n\nIndeed lambda<5/2, u<=1/28 imply\n2/[lambda*(1+u)]>112/145>3/4 and lambda*u<5/56<1/10;\nthus Delta/u>13/20>1/2. Dropping the negative term and lambda>1\ngives the upper bound. In particular2<M<5/2, 1<s<3/2 and\nE=M+Delta. Write Bgeo=M+E=2M+Delta<6; Bgeo is NOT B_n.\n\nThe accepted benchmark bound at the ACTUAL horizon gives\n\n    B_n<=4(n+1)^rho=4lambda^m.                       (3)\n\nFor clarity its direct all-split step is\nG(x,4y)<=4Omega(x,y); induction yields B_n<=4C_n, and the\naccepted elementary Omega power bound yields C_n<=(n+1)^rho.\nBalanced splitting is not assumed for B_n.\n\nCombining(1) with c>lambda and lambda^m>2^m>=128 gives\n\n    1+2S_m>(9/14)lambda^m-1>(1/2)lambda^m,\n    B_n/(1+2S_m)<8.                                 (4)\n\nThe second strict mass inequality follows from lambda^m/7>1.\nNo asymptotic profile constant or executed recurrence is needed.\n\n## 3. Policy1: static preserving curvature with an actual off-line step\n\nEvery parameter in this policy is specified from the known full word\nBEFORE running GD. Define\n\n    d=a/[2(1+beta)], p=e1,\n    q=-d*e1+sqrt(a*d-d^2)*e2, Q=||q||^2=a*d,\n    k=beta/(2M), r=-k*q+k*sqrt(Q)*e3,\n    R=||r||^2=2k^2 Q,\n    y=-a*e1-q,\n    z=y-beta*q-M*r=y-(beta/2)*(q+sqrt(Q)*e3),\n    tR=z+s*r.\n\nThe radicand is positive since0<d<a. Direct products give\n\n    y.q=y.r=z.r=0, z.q=-beta*Q/2,\n    q.r=-kQ, tR.r=sR,\n    y_1=-a+d<0,\n    z_1=-a+(1+beta/2)d<0,\n    (tR)_1=-a+(1+beta-k)d<-a/2.\n\nThe auxiliary homogeneous reference is the SINGLE static function\n\n    Dentry=conv{0,p,q,r},\n    Fentry(x)=max_(g in Dentry){x.g-||g||^2/2},\n    Jentry(w)=max(0,p.w,q.w,r.w).\n\nIts gradient is Euclidean projection onto Dentry. A pair x=w+g\nhas that gradient iff g belongs to Dentry and\nw.b<=w.g for every b in Dentry. Then\nFentry(x)=Jentry(w)+||g||^2/2. The following exhaustive guards\nderive its four ORIGINAL entry jets, without adopting the prior draft:\n\n| query | actual x | gradient | proximal w | value | every support guard |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n|0|e1|p|0|1/2|all four planes have height0; x=p is in Dentry|\n|1|-a e1=y+q|q|y|Q/2|zero,q,r tie at0; p.y<0|\n|2|y-beta q=tR+r|r|tR|sR+R/2|r height sR>0; q height=(-beta+k)Q<0; p height<0; zero0|\n|3|z+r|r|z|R/2|zero,r tie0; q.z<0; p.z<0|\n\nThe actual first steps are(b_0,b_1,b_0,b_2), so these reach\nx_4=z-E r. Projection uniqueness resolves all support ties.\nFor any two such reference jets the FULL ordered residual cancels to\n\n    Q_ij=(x_j-g_j).(g_i-g_j)<=0. Thus the conjugate\nconstruction of accepted1252 applies to the complete entry.\n\nThe entire old four-query anchor list is precisely\n\n    (anchor,offset)=(0,0),(y,0),(z,0),(tR,sR). The FIRST r query is tR with POSITIVE offset sR.\nThe q-stage has no unselected step, so its first and last anchors\ncoincide at y. These are not deleted or replaced by terminal guesses.\n\nFreeze the following SINGLE law before any update:\n\n    eta=8(1+S_m), T=1+eta,\n    Heta(v)=||v||^2/2+max(0,y.v,z.v,tR.v-sR)\n                         +(eta/2)*(v_3)_-^2,\n    Feta(x)=sup_v{x.v-Heta(v)}, x_0=e1.             (7)\n\nThe four max-affine offsets are0,0,0,-sR, all weights1. The last\nterm is an explicit convex C1 one-sided quadratic. This changes the\nfunction selected BEFORE GD. No function is changed later.\n\nAt each promised entry gradient p,q,r,r its penalty and its derivative\nare0, since the third coordinates are0,0,k*sqrt(Q),k*sqrt(Q).\nThe unchanged entry conjugate conditions are\nx_0=p+0, x_1=q+y, x_2=r+tR, x_3=r+z. The exact four values also\nsurvive. Consequently the actual run of Feta reaches the displayed\nx_4=z-E r using every original entry step.\n\n### 3.1 Global regularity, minimum, radius and all interpolation rows\n\nHeta is finite continuous1-strongly convex with quadratic growth.\nEvery x has a unique conjugate maximizer g, characterized by\nx in partial Heta(g). This is the direct constructive\nargument from accepted1252, with the additional explicit convex term.\n\nAt v=0 the penalty derivative is0. The active affine slopes are\nEXACTLY0,y,z, since the tR term has strictly negative offset.\nTherefore the COMPLETE minimum set and minimum value are\n\n    Ceta=conv{0,y,z}, min Feta=0.
